  2. Rather than hardcoding the IP address into the script, edit your banner-grabbing tool so that it prompts the user for the IP address.
  3. Edit your tcp_server.py to prompt the user for the port to listen on.
  4. Build the FTPcracker in Listing 17-7 and then edit it to use a word list for user variables (similar what we did with the password) rather than prompting the user for input.
  5. Add an except clause to the banner-grabbing tool that prints “no answer” if the port is closed.

18ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE FOR HACKERS

As you know, we’re on the brink of an artificial intelligence revolution! Artificial intelligence is growing by leaps and bounds, and it’s beginning to integrate into our everyday lives and cybersecurity in various ways. AI will likely change our lives and our work in ways that are hard to anticipate.

Do not fret, I can tell you one thing with certainty: AI will not make cybersecurity engineers obsolete. Artificial intelligence will make you better at your job if you embrace it and learn to use it properly and efficiently.

Here are a few reasons AI will not replace all cybersecurity professionals:

  • AI Augments Rather Than Replaces Cybersecurity Roles
  • AI can automate certain routine tasks and enhance threat detection capabilities, but it can’t fully replace the critical thinking, creativity, and decision-making skills of human cybersecurity professionals. AI lacks the intuition, context understanding, and ability to devise the innovative solutions that are essential in cybersecurity.
  • AI Has Limitations
  • AI algorithms are trained on existing data and may struggle to detect new or sophisticated cyber threats that haven’t been seen before. Cyber-criminals can create attacks designed to bypass AI systems, necessitating human intervention and ingenuity to respond effectively. In this circumstance, AI will necessitate additional, well-trained cybersecurity professionals.
  • Cybersecurity Requires Human Expertise
  • Cybersecurity is an ever-evolving field that demands continuous learning (see the Hackers-Arise Lifetime training program), adaptation, and ethical decision-making. While AI can assist in automating tasks, it can’t replace the human expertise required to understand the context, evaluate the effectiveness of security measures, and make decisions based on legal and ethical considerations (see the Hackers-Arise Criminal Law for Cybersecurity Professionals training program).
  • New Roles Will Emerge
  • As AI becomes more prevalent in cybersecurity, new roles and specializations will emerge, such as AI security analysts, machine learning security engineers, and professionals responsible for developing, training, implementing, and securing AI systems. These roles will complement and work alongside traditional cybersecurity roles. These positions will likely have even higher salaries than the traditional cybersecurity engineers.

Collaboration Is Key

The most effective approach to leveraging AI revolution in cybersecurity will be the collaboration between AI and human cybersecurity professionals. AI can augment human capabilities by automating routine tasks, enabling professionals to focus on more complex and strategic aspects of cybersecurity. Humans will oversee and guide AI systems to ensure ethical and effective cybersecurity practices.

While AI will transform and enhance various aspects of cybersecurity, it’s highly unlikely to make human cybersecurity engineers obsolete in the next 5 to 10 years. The dynamic nature of cyber threats, the limitations of AI, and the need for human expertise and ethical decision-making will ensure that cybersecurity remains a field that requires skilled professionals working in tandem with AI technologies.

Major Players in AI

Although the field is advancing rapidly, the first mass-market commercial-level AI, ChatGPT, was introduced in November 2022 from OpenAI. Since then, many companies have developed and released their own unique AI models, including:

  • Copilot from Microsoft
  • Grok from X (formerly Twitter)
  • Gemini from Google
  • Perplexity from Perplexity AI
  • Claude 2 by Anthropic
  • Llama 2 from Meta

Using AI in Cybersecurity

As I mentioned, there are several ways that a cybersecurity professional can leverage AI in their job. For instance, one of the key tasks in cybersecurity is known as cyber threat intelligence, which is the practice of staying abreast of the latest threats in cybersecurity. To do just that, for example, I could ask my favorite AI, “What are the latest threats in cybersecurity?” It might answer:

Ransomware attacks: These continue to be a major threat, with cybercriminals encrypting data and demanding payment for decryption keys.

Cloud vulnerabilities: As more organizations rely on cloud services, securing cloud environments has become critical. Cloud vulnerabilities have increased 150% in the last five years.

Mobile device vulnerabilities: With increased remote work and BYOD policies, mobile devices are increasingly targeted by cybercriminals.

AI-powered attacks: Cybercriminals are leveraging AI and machine learning to develop more sophisticated attacks and evade detection.

Social engineering and phishing: These remain prevalent, with 85% of data breaches involving human interaction.

IoT device vulnerabilities: As IoT adoption grows, so do the security risks associated with these often poorly secured devices.

Supply chain attacks: Compromising software or hardware before it reaches the consumer is an increasing concern.

Insider threats: Both accidental and malicious insider threats continue to pose significant risks.

Advanced persistent threats (APTs): These stealthy, long-term attacks aimed at specific targets are becoming more common.

Cryptocurrency-related attacks: As cryptocurrencies gain popularity, attacks targeting these digital assets are on the rise.

In this way, I can stay ahead of potential threats to my organization.

In addition, I could be even more specific and ask my favorite AI, “What are the latest ransomware threats?” It might answer:

LockBit: This remains one of the most active and dangerous ransomware groups, despite recent law enforcement disruptions. They continue to evolve their tactics and target a wide range of organizations.

RansomHub: This group emerged suddenly in 2024, possibly as a reincarnation of the AlphV/BlackCat group. They quickly became one of the most active ransomware threats.

Akira: This ransomware group has risen to prominence, becoming one of the top threats in 2024.

Play: A relatively new Linux version of this ransomware has been observed, expanding its potential target base.

Cactus: This group has been responsible for high-profile attacks, including one on a Swedish supermarket chain.

Medusa: They’ve been active in targeting various sectors, including public institutions.

8Base: This group has claimed responsibility for attacks on several organizations, including the United Nations Development Programme.

Daixin Team: They’ve been involved in attacks on healthcare providers, claiming to have stolen large amounts of patient data.

Social Engineering Attacks with AI

Over 80 percent of all successful cyberattacks include an element of social engineering. Social engineering relies upon manipulating human vulnerabilities rather than technical vulnerabilities. Tactics like phishing, email impersonation, and pretexting are becoming increasingly sophisticated and prevalent. For instance, ransomware attacks, presently the most successful financial attacks, usually begin with someone within the targeted organization opening a file or clicking a link. This simple action gives that attacker an opening into the organization, allowing them to encrypt all the data in the organization, which leads to the crippling of the organization. Eventually, the company must pay the ransom to get its data back.

In the initial phases of AI in cybersecurity, attackers will likely focus on using AI to craft email messages that entice their targets to click a link or open a document. Writing email may be one of the best uses for artificial intelligence for hackers right now, as AI can gather the key information and draft an excellent and effective email to the intended targets.

Let’s ask our favorite AI to write an email to OccupyTheWeb to purchase the translation rights to the video companion to this book (this is a real-life social engineering attack where I was a target). The key is that AI can write email messages that are highly targeted and tailored to the individual recipient. Here’s its draft:

Subject: Offer to Purchase Rights to “Linux Basics for Hackers” training videos

Dear OccupyTheWeb Team,

I am writing to express my strong interest in acquiring the full rights and ownership of your popular “Linux Basics for Hackers” online book. As an experienced cybersecurity professional and educator, I have followed your work closely and been extremely impressed by the quality and depth of content in this Linux fundamentals course tailored for ethical hackers. The hands-on virtual machine labs, comprehensive video lessons, and real-world examples make this one of the best resources available for mastering Linux from an offensive security perspective. I believe that under my ownership, I can invest in expanding and updating this course to ensure it remains the industry-leading program for this topic. My team and I have extensive experience developing and marketing online cybersecurity training, and we have the resources to translate this course into multiple languages to greatly increase its reach globally. I would be willing to pay a competitive one-time fee to purchase the permanent full rights, or potentially explore a revenue share model if you prefer. Of course, I am open to discussing any terms or conditions you may have as well. The “Linux Basics for Hackers” course is a fantastic product that deserves more exposure and growth, which I feel I could provide with my resources and industry expertise. Please let me know if you would be interested in further discussing a sale or potential partnership. Thank you for your consideration. I look forward to hearing from you.

Sincerely,[Your Name]

As you can see, this is a very professional and convincing email in contrast to the typical badly spelled and grammatically lacking “Nigerian Prince” email we have all received. This message is not only well written but also incorporates information gained from open source internet sources about the target and the subject.

Using AI to Write a Bash Script

In Chapter 8, we looked at some basics of bash scripting. Bash scripting can make your life so much more efficient, as you can write a small script to do repetitive tasks that a machine does much better. You also can use AI to write these scripts, even if your scripting skills are limited.

For instance, let’s ask our favorite AI application to write a script to use a cron job to back up our data every Saturday night at 11:30 PM (presumably when no one is on the network). Backing up your data is your best prevention from the damage done by ransomware. Here’s the script:

#!/bin/bash

# Set the source directory to backup
SOURCE_DIR="/path/to/your/data"

# Set the backup destination
BACKUP_DIR="/path/to/backup/location"

# Create a timestamp for the backup file
TIMESTAMP=$(date +"%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")

# Set the backup filename
BACKUP_FILE="backup_${TIMESTAMP}.tar.gz"

# Create the backup directory if it doesn't exist
mkdir -p "$BACKUP_DIR"

# Create the backup
tar -czf "${BACKUP_DIR}/${BACKUP_FILE}" "$SOURCE_DIR"

# Optional: Remove backups older than 30 days
find "$BACKUP_DIR" -name "backup_*.tar.gz" -mtime +30 -delete

# Optional: Log the backup operation
echo "Backup completed on $(date)" >> "${BACKUP_DIR}/backup_log.txt"

The AI then goes on to remind us to change the permissions:

chmod +x /path/to/backup_script.sh
then edit our cron table
crontab -e
30 23 * * 6 /path/to/backup_script.sh

Now, we should be able to back up our system automatically. Isn’t AI wonderful?

Summary

Our digital world continues to evolve with new and better applications and devices. Artificial intelligence is another advance along this long road. Just as businesses that refused to use email or build websites have disappeared into the dustbin of history, you must adapt and embrace this new technology. It can make you so much better at what you do!
